## Document 027: 20261015【投研報告】市場快訊_通膨升溫壓抑美股表現，近期聚焦財報結果

通膨升溫壓抑美股表現，近期聚焦財報結果
2026 年 10 月 15 日
一、 市場動態
1. 科技股領跌，費半連 6 日收黑：美股主要指數 10 月 14 日全數下跌，道瓊工業指數下
跌 0.09%，收 51,621.48 點；S&P 500 指數下跌 1.11%，收 7,718.94 點，為 7 月 29
日以來最大單日跌幅；Nasdaq 綜合指數下跌 1.88%，收 26,692.87 點，為 7 月 23
日以來最大單日跌幅。費城半導體指數下跌 4.30%，收 11,684.38 點，連續第６個交
易日走低，為 2025 年 5 月以來最長連跌紀錄。

2. 類股非全面下挫，資金仍呈輪動現象：半導體及 AI 相關股票在 10/14 跌幅尤其明顯
，AMD 下跌 5.73%、Nvidia 下跌 3.16%、Broadcom 下跌 3.10%，AI 基礎建設相關
的 CoreWeave、Nebius、Oracle 及 Dell 跌幅更深，顯示市場對資本支出規模較大、
融資成本敏感度較高的 AI 相關企業風險偏好明顯下降。不過，S&P 500 的 11 大類
股中有 8 類下跌，資訊科技及通訊服務為主要拖累來源，但醫療保健、必需消費與能
源類股仍逆勢上漲。
3. 美國 10 年期公債殖利率盤中急升後回落：美國 10 年期公債殖利率 10/14 由 5.2%左
右快速升至約 5.33%，逼近近期高點；然而，隨後市場消化 Fed 官員較審慎的政策訊
號，殖利率自高檔回落，終場約收於 5.19%，較前一交易日 5.21%小幅下降約 2 個基
點。
二、 下跌原因分析
1. 美 9 月通膨高於預期 ，Fed 再升息風險重新升高 ：美國 10/14 公布 9 月 CPI 月增 0.6%

、年增率也由 8 月的 3.4%上揚至 3.7%；核心 CPI 月增 0.3%、年增率由 8 月的 2.4%
升至 2.6%。其中汽油價格單月大漲 9.1%，貢獻整體 CPI 月增幅超過一半；住宅成本
亦月增 0.4%，顯示物價壓力仍具黏性。此數據公布後，市場重新提高 Fed 進一步升
息的預期 ，CME FedWatch 所反映的 10 月升息 1 碼機率一度由前一日約 22%提高至
37%。雖然截至 10/15 台北時間早上 7 點左右該機率回降至 29.3%，這也提醒投資人
Fed 的升息路徑仍存在不確定性。
2. 高利率同時壓縮科技股估值與提高 AI 融資成本 ：科技及半導體公司未來獲利成長占評
價的比重較高，因此對折現率變化相對敏感。當長天期美債殖利率維持 5%以上，市
場願意支付的本益比自然受到限制，即使企業獲利仍維持成長，股價也可能因評價倍
數下降而出現修正。另一方面，本輪 AI 投資需要大量資料中心、伺服器、電力與網路
基礎設施，高利率亦直接增加企業融資成本，反映信用市場對高度依賴外部資金的 AI

基礎建設公司要求更高風險溢酬。

策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
3. AI 投資焦點由「支出規模」轉向「投資報酬率」:近期市場對 AI 的疑慮逐漸由「需求
是否成長」 ，轉向「龐大的資本支出能否轉化為足夠營收、獲利與自由現金流」 。
Broadcom 執行長陳福陽近期亦指出 ，AI 未來發展的重要限制可能逐漸由晶片或記憶
體供應轉向資本本身。我們認為，這並不代表AI 資本支出週期即將結束，而是市場已
由第一階段的 「資本支出愈大、成長想像愈高」 ，逐步進入更重視資本效率與投資報酬
率的第二階段。未來財務結構、現金流與AI 商業化能力，將成為不同企業評價分化的
重要因素。
4. 中國記憶體競爭疑慮加重半導體短線壓力：中國長鑫存儲（CXMT）10/10 傳出記憶

體技術取得進展 ， 引發市場對未來DRAM 供給增加及價格競爭的疑慮 ， 記憶體相關類
股率先承壓，並進一步影響整體半導體市場情緒。惟 DRAM 與 GPU、客製化 ASIC、
先進晶圓製程及先進封裝的產業競爭與需求結構並不相同。我們認為，儘管記憶體競
爭升溫值得關注，但目前尚不足以延伸為整體 AI 半導體需求同步轉弱。
5. 高檔籌碼加速獲利了結：AI 與半導體仍是 2026 年美股表現最強勢的投資主題之一，
部分科技股今年累計漲幅已相當可觀。當通膨、殖利率及AI 投資報酬疑慮同時升高，
先前漲幅較大、投資人部位較集中的族群自然成為優先調節對象。因此，本波費半快
速修正除了總經與基本面疑慮外，亦包含籌碼降溫及風險部位收縮因素，使短期價格
波動幅度可能高於企業基本面實際變化。
三、市場看法
1. 類股輪動明顯，尚非全面看空美股：科技與 AI 相關股票 10 月 14 日明顯承壓，但醫
療保健、必需消費與能源類股仍逆勢上漲，道瓊工業指數僅下跌 0.09%。此外，S&P
500 成分股下跌家數與上漲家數之比約為 1.1：1；相較於指數下跌 1.11%，市場廣度

並未同步大幅惡化。這反映資金主要由高評價且前期漲幅較大的科技與AI 族群，轉向
現金流較穩定的防禦型產業。現階段市場特徵較接近類股輪動與風格切換，而非無差
別的全面風險撤離，亦尚未看到美股企業獲利或經濟基本面全面反轉的明確訊號。
2. 美股科技股財報前風險降載，Q3 仍存在重新定價空間：近期科技股修正，費城半導
體指數連續６個交易日下跌，也可能部分反映第三季財報季正式展開前，投資人在股
價先前漲幅較大、評價偏高及利率環境不確定下，提前降低風險曝險。真正決定後續
市場方向的關鍵 ， 仍在於實際企業獲利能否支持目前的高成長預期 。根據 Bloomberg
季度獲利成長追蹤資料，今年 Q1 與 Q2 財報季期間的 EPS 成長率經驗顯示，隨實際
財報陸續公布，企業獲利成長曾明顯優於財報季開始前的市場預期。儘管這並不保證
Q3 將複製前兩季的上修幅度，但目前市場因財報不確定性所反映的風險折價仍存在
被實際財報重新驗證的可能。若大型科技及半導體公司後續營收、獲利與AI 需求指引
實際公布結果優於預期，近期風險溢價可望部分收斂；反之，若獲利成長無法跟上目

前評價，市場整理時間則可能進一步延長。

不另行通知。
3. 費半修正幅度大，但 AI 需求尚未出現全面反轉：ASML 在 10/14 所公布的最新第三
季財報反而顯示 AI 相關設備需求仍相當強勁，該公司表示 2027 與 2028 年規劃產能
可能仍無法完全滿足客戶需求，部分客戶亦已表達對 2029 及 2030 年設備需求的興
趣。儘管費城半導體指數近期市場情緒轉弱，但由 ASML 的財報表現來看，目前仍未
看到大型 AI 客戶全面取消晶片訂單、主要雲端服務商同步削減 AI 資本支出，或先進
製程需求快速降溫等結構性訊號。
4. 台積電營收顯示 AI 需求暢旺 ， 留意10/15 法說會內容：台積電 10/8 公布的第三季營

收達新台幣 1.49 兆元、年增約 50%，創歷史新高，並高於市場原先預估的 1.46 兆元
；9 月單月營收年增 54.6%。強勁的營收表現仍反映 AI 與先進製程需求具有支撐。目
前市場因此呈現「企業營收仍快速成長，但股票評價下降」的狀況。高殖利率環境下
，EPS 上升與本益比下降可以同時發生，後續股價方向將取決於企業獲利上修幅度是
否足以抵銷評價收縮。該公司將於 10/15 下午舉行第三季法人說明會。市場重點將集
中於第四季展望、AI 與 HPC 需求、先進製程、先進封裝及未來資本支出規劃。公司
對 AI 需求與產能投資的最新判斷，將成為判斷近期費半修正主要屬於評價調整，或產
業基本面是否開始出現變化的重要依據。
5. AI 與半導體進入基本面分化階段：我們認為，在高利率環境下，高度仰賴外部融資、
自由現金流尚未成熟的 AI 基礎建設業者 ， 受到的資金成本壓力明顯高於具穩定現金流
的大型科技與雲端服務商。同樣地，記憶體、GPU、ASIC、先進製程及先進封裝各有
不同供需結構。我們預期下一階段 AI 行情將更加重視訂單能見度、產品競爭力、毛利

率、自由現金流及 AI 營收實際兌現，產業內部強弱分化程度可能進一步提高。
6. Fed 路徑仍存不確定性，惟企業獲利可望提供股市支撐：美國 9 月 CPI 升溫使市場重
新評估 Fed 後續升息時點與幅度，美國利率政策仍存在不確定性，長天期美債殖利率
也可能維持高檔。不過，現階段企業獲利成長與AI 相關需求仍具韌性，若第三季財報

策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
延續前兩季優於預期的趨勢，企業 EPS 若能上修有望部分抵銷高利率對本益比的壓縮
效果。換言之，後續市場表現未必只由 Fed 政策主導，企業實際獲利能否持續兌現，
將是支撐科技與半導體股中期走勢的重要力量。
四、投資建議
1. 短線聚焦美股財報與 Fed 決策，科技股中期趨勢料不致有變：近期市場同時面臨財報
前的觀望態勢、美債殖利率偏高及 AI 資本支出疑慮等不確定因素，短線風險偏好可能
受到壓抑，後續宜關注 10 月美股財報對企業獲利與資本支出展望，以及 FOMC 會議
所釋出的政策訊號。中期而言，目前AI 相關企業營運與資本支出尚未出現全面轉弱跡

象，科技產業基本面仍具一定支撐，但在利率維持高檔及市場評價偏高的環境下，後
續宜更加重視企業獲利、現金流及資本支出的實際變化。
2. 中天期債券風險報酬仍較均衡 ：美國 9 月 CPI 升溫使短端殖利率持續受到 Fed 政策路
徑影響，而 20～30 年超長端仍面臨財政赤字、國債供給與期限溢價偏高等結構性壓
力。相較之下，目前殖利率已位於歷史相對高檔，若後續核心通膨或能源價格改善，
中段殖利率具有回落條件，同時其存續期間風險又低於超長端，因此現階段以中天期
美國公債及投資級債的風險報酬結構相對較佳。

## Document 108: 261012_ubs_largan

ab 12 October 2026
Global Research
Largan Precision
Core weakness outweighs progress in AI optics
CPO progress is more tangible but substantial success is already priced in
We maintain Sell and our NT$5,000 target price as erosion in the core smartphone
business outweighs CPO progress. The Q326 call confirmed that Largan's fiber array has
passed customer qualification although its customer qualified another vendor's prism
micro-lens assembly (PMLA) that Largan would now buy in. If Largan qualifies the full

FAU plus carrier assembly for 2H27, content rises but with more pass-through cost and
less margin upside. We roll our valuation to 2028E, the first full year of CPO production,
though 25% below the NT$6,660 close on October 8.
Full FAU assembly would lift content, but with more pass-through
We believe Largan's FA customer is one of two  module suppliers on NVIDIA's 2H27
program, which we estimate could reach 40% share. The program is still subject to

successful yield ramp and could move to metalens in 2028, which would reopen supplier
qualification. Assembling the full FAU could lift Largan's ASP to US$125-150 leveraging
its precision alignment and automation but the PMLA, V-groove and carrier parts would
be pass through that cuts into margin leverage. We expand our FAU TAM analysis
(Figure 9S u m m a r y  o f  F A U  d r i v e r s :  1 2 . 3 m n / 3 5 . 6 m n  b a s e  c a s e  i n  2 0 2 8 - 2 0 3 0) with FAU market in 2028/30 expanding to 12.3mn/35.6mn units, with Largan

base case set at 25% of the TAM.
Core smartphone business stays soft into 2027
Q326 sales of NT$15.7bn, +15% QoQ/-11% YoY missedUBSe/Street +36%/+33% on
weaker flagship smartphone demand. GMs fell 7.2ppts QoQ to 42.2%, lowest in 13
years on renewable energy costs and low variable-aperture yields, dropping OpM to 17
year lows at 30.3% and EPS to NT$39.6, below UBS/street NT$51.50/NT$49.69. We cut
Q4 to NT$16.7bn +7% QoQ, 11% below consensus as its customer trimmed orders

after a pricier iPhone 18 launch and possible lag to Genius which is picking up periscope
share and outgrew in Q3 (+1% YoY vs. -11% YoY). We cut 2027 from NT$77bn to NT
$67bn, before a recovery to NT$79bn in 2028 with the next periscope upgrade. We see
GM recovering to 47.5%/48.9% in 2027-28 as yields improve and energy costs pass
through in 2028 pricing.
Maintain Sell and our NT$5,000 target
We cut 2026E/27E EPS by 14%/16% to NT$160/NT$180 and introduce 2028E EPS of

NT$220 for the core smartphone business. We value the lens business at NT$3,300 (15x
2028E ex-CPO EPS of NT$220, the middle of Largan's 13–18x historical range) and CPO
option at NT$1,700 or US$7bn (49x our base-case 2028E CPO EPS of NT$35, near the
top of optical peers' 14–54x). Our base case gives Largan 25% of a 12.3mn-unit, US
$1.85bn FAU market at US$150 per unit and a 50% gross margin. That credits it with
yields and margins it still needs to prove in a quarter when yields and energy costs cut

7.2ppts from its lens margin. At ~NT$6,660, the shares imply ~NT$69 of 2028E CPO
EPS, about twice our base case.

CPO fiber array qualified, but rising pass-through as it upgrades to FAU
production
Largan's fiber array (FA) has passed qualification with a volume customer, and
management expects mass-production capacity by mid-2027 in a newly completed fab
dedicated to FA/FAU, using in-house equipment. The company's next step is to produce
the full detachable FAU mounted on a carrier, a board with three alignment beams that
sits on the optical engine's receptor. Largan can build FAUs now and says the customer

### Page 3

would prefer it to supply the full assembly, but qualification is pending with the
customer preferring FAU and carrier sampled separately.
Even as its potential to supply the full FAU improve, Largan will buy in most of the
content. The customer has qualified another PMLA vendor and prefers semiconductor-
made optics; V-grooves and carrier beams and boards are outsourced and the customer
does not want Largan to make MT ferrules. Management described its role as closer to a

contractor and said margins hinge on yields, as the FA carries a high ASP but also a large
bill of materials.  Supplying the complete FAU could raise billed content, but a larger
purchased bill of materials also dilutes the relationship between revenue and value
added. Our full-FAU ASP assumption requires Largan to capture that invoice scope.
Next year's product is grating-coupled and works for both CPO and NPO while edge-
coupled designs are being explored with the customer. The current product does not go

directly to end customers, but management expects to work directly with end customers
on next-generation designs (likely 2028) as it progresses toward multi-row FAs.
To ramp production, Largan has bought several land parcels and will build new fabs from
scratch, with construction finishing around 2030. It may outsource some capacity in the
interim and said it cannot yet be sure capacity will meet demand.
Annual FAU TAM reaches US$1.85bn in 2028 and US$4.5bn in 2030 in

our base case
We update our FAU (fiber array unit) TAM factoring CPO/NPO penetration into
AI networking switches, NVIDIA multi-rack systems, scale-up on compute and switch
boards, and non-NVIDIA accelerators.  We assume one passive FAU assembly connecting
a fiber array to a photonic engine with one unit per optical engine.
We assume one FAU per optical engine and 32 per switch ASIC in our base case,
matching NVIDIA's 32x3.2T engines on its 102.4T Spectrum-X package. Broadcom's

102.4Tb/s Davisson uses 16 engines at 6.4Tb/s, so our bear case assumes and even mix
of these two designs (24 per ASIC). We note that higher switch bandwidth does not
automatically increase FAU count if engine bandwidth rises at the same rate. Our 2030
assumption maintains the 32 FAUs per switch based on bandwidth rising to 204.8Tb/s
ASICs using 6.4Tb/s optical engines.

Our base case for the FAU market sums up 4 opportunities for CPO/NPO attach:
1. Scale-out and scale-across switches
We estimate annual switch ASIC shipments x CPO + NPO penetration x optical engines
per ASIC x FAUs per engine. In 2028, 25% CPO/NPO penetration or 1.0m 102.4T switch
ASICs at 32 FAUs each produces 8.0m FAUs. In 2030, 55% of 1.1mn 204.8T switch
ASICs at 32 FAUs drives a market size of 19.4mn FAUs.

2. NVIDIA's multi-rack connections for Rubin Ultra and Feynman
NVIDIA at its 2026 GTC San Jose proposed a multi-rack option to network together 8
Oberon NVL72 racks for the Rubin Ultra generation and 8 Kyber NVL144 racks.
Feedback at the time showed  possible transceiver or NPO connections for Rubin Ultra

and CPO connections for Feynman.  Our 2028 base case uses 110,000 racks, 15% multi-
rack adoption and 75% CPO/NPO penetration, giving approximately 12,400 connected
racks.
For 2030, our rack count falls to 35,000 racks as we assume Kyber would have 4x the
number of GPUs, equivalent to 140k Oberon equivalents. We assume the 35,000 racks
at 55% multi-rack adoption x 80% CPO/NPO attach gives 15,400 connected racks. We
assume about 216 and 576 inter-rack FAUs per rack, respectively, driving a market

opportunity of 2.7mn FAUs in 2028 and 8.9mn in 2030.

Overall market opportunity: 12.3mn/35.6mn generates a US$1.85bn/US
$4.5bn TAM in 2028/2030
We sum four pools: scale-out/scale-across switches, NVIDIA multi-rack pods, CPO native

NVIDIA GPUs and non-NVIDIA scale-up.  We add 10%/5% for FAUs lost at downstream
attach and test and for field spares, driving a market size of in our base case of 12.3mn
FAUs in 2028 and 35.6mn in 2030. At US$150/US$125 per FAU, that is a US$1.85bn
market in 2028 and US$4.5bn in 2030 (bear/bull US$0.5bn/US$3.5bn in 2028; US
$1.4bn/US$9.0bn in 2030). The expansion to 2030 comes from wider optical adoption
and greater content in scale-up systems, despite an assumed FAU ASP decline from US

$150 to US$125.

Scenario analysis on Largan's CPO opportunity
Our base case gives Largan the benefit of the doubt for successfully ramping up a full
FAU production and capturing 25% of the overall FAU TAM, yielding NT$35 of 2028E
EPS, about 16% of our 2028E lens EPS. A 25% share means 3.1mn FAUs in its first full
year of production: about 29% of NVIDIA-linked units and roughly three-quarters of the

allocation our checks suggest its module partner holds. We book these at the full US
$150 FAU price and 50% gross margin, allowing for an early-supply premium even
though the optics, V-grooves and carrier parts are bought in. That gives 2028E CPO sales
of US$462mn, rising to US$1.1bn and NT$84 of EPS in 2030 on a larger market.
Our bear case (15% share, 45% GM) gives NT$5 of CPO EPS in 2028E and NT$14 in
2030E; our bull case (35% share, 55% GM) gives NT$103 and NT$267. Each 5ppt of

share moves 2028E CPO EPS by ~NT$6.9 (~NT$340/share at 49x), and each 5ppt of
gross margin by ~NT$4.6 (~NT$225/share).  If gross margin were 35% rather than 50%,
holding operating expenses at 12% of sales, EPS would fall to approximately NT$21. A
next-generation shift in lens or coupling design could also change content and
alignment requirements including a potential metalens design which could invite in
different suppliers.
Our 2030 base case implies ~8.9mn Largan FAUs a year (21mn in the upside case),

though with risks around competition in a crowded field, yields and technology. A shift
to semiconductor-made optics or metalens would loosen alignment tolerances and
erode the precision premium behind Largan's pitch. The upside lies in direct work with
cloud providers on multi-row FAs from 2028, where Largan's multi-layer alignment is
most differentiated.

Valuation and investment implications
We roll our valuation forward to 2028E and value Largan on a sum of the parts. We
value the lens business at NT$3,300 (15x 2028E EPS, the middle of Largan's 13–18x

historical range) and CPO at NT$1,700 (49x our base-case 2028E CPO EPS of NT$35,
near the top of optical peers' 14–54x). Our base case gives Largan 25% of a 12.3mn-
unit, US$1.85bn FAU market at US$150 per unit and a 50% gross margin. The 49x
multiple also assigns a substantial value to a business whose complete assembly still
requires qualification and whose margins still need to demonstrate can reach similar
levels to its mature smartphone business. At ~NT$6,660 recent share price level, the

shares imply ~NT$69 of 2028E CPO EPS, about twice our base case.
Upside risks include faster full-FAU qualification, firm volume commitments from
additional customers, stronger lens demand and better yields. Near-term catalysts are
the customer's capacity forecast and its visit to the new fab in the coming month. A
large forecast or faster FAU plus carrier qualification would be upside risks to our sell.

Forecast returns
Forecast price appreciation -24.9%
Forecast dividend yield 1.2%
Forecast stock return -23.7%
Market return assumption 6.3%
Forecast excess return -30.0%
Company Description
Founded in 1987, Largan Precision (Largan) is one of the world's leading manufacturers of
lenses used in cameras and IT products. It specialises in plastic/hybrid lenses and, to a lesser
extent, glass lenses and modules. In 2022, 20MP+ camera lenses were 10-20% of Largan's

shipment mix, 10MP lenses were 50-60%, 8MP lenses were 0-10% and other lenses were
30-40%.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
Our price target is based on SOTP valuation on the CPO opportunity and traditional lens
business.
We think Largan's downside risks include: 1) ongoing pricing pressure from smartphone OEM
clients and increasingly intense competition among lens suppliers; and 2) a slower-than-
expected smartphone demand recovery in China, and 3) CPO commercial success below the

market's elevated expectations.
We believe the key upside risk is a faster-than-expected CPO qualification process at Largan.
In addition, if the company demonstrates exceptionally efficient and highly accurate
automation capabilities, it could support greater market share gains within the industry than
we currently anticipate.

KEY DEFINITIONS:Forecast Stock Return (FSR) is defined as expected percentage price appreciation plus gross dividend yield over
the next 12 months. In some cases, this yield may be based on accrued dividends. Market Return Assumption (MRA) is defined as
the one-year local market interest rate plus 5% (a proxy for, and not a forecast of, the equity risk premium). Under Review (UR)
Stocks may be flagged as UR by the analyst, indicating that the stock's price target and/or rating are subject to possible change in the

near term, usually in response to an event that may affect the investment case or valuation. Equity Price Targets have an investment
horizon of 12 months.

such as structure, management, performance record, discount; Neutral: Neutral on factors such as structure, management,
performance record, discount; Sell: Negative on factors such as structure, management, performance record, discount. Core

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

considerations may be lower or higher than portfolios where ESG factors, exclusions, or other sustainability issues are not considered by the portfolio manager, and the
investment opportunities available to such portfolios may differ.

OCP Wrap-up: Rising Optics and
Al Infrastructure Alternatives
Our US trip highlighted ICA/IPO traction, complementary rack-
management silicon, hardware security and CXL-based memory
pooling, with potential storage server upside.

Greater China Technology Semiconductors
Asia Pacific
Industry View Attractive
ICA/IPO: Gaining traction, but cost still a hurdle. A China CSP outlined optical
form factors from XPO/NPO to interposer optics (IPO) and CPO, including

integration of IPO with interposer copper attach (ICA). TE Connectivity showcased
224G/448G NPO/CPO sockets and ICA sockets with 0.4mm pitch and up to 10,000
pins, offering high-density connectivity, low crosstalk and improved reworkability.
We see rising traction for ICA/IPO, with potential mass production in 2027   (For
more, see our recent CPO report).
Marvell IMC: Complementary to AST2700, not a replacement. Marvell
demonstrated the OCTEON-based infrastructure management controller (IMC)

addresses higher-level Al rack/cluster telemetry and control; ASPEED's AST2700
BMC continues to handle node-level security and remote management. Given
distinct functions and software ecosystems, we do not view IMC as a direct
AST2700 competitor. We also don't see an AST2700 production issue, supporting
an on-track ramp. The key debate is actually market share in TPU/Axion.
Caliptra: Expanding hardware Root of Trust opportunities. The open Caliptra

framework defines silicon-level Root of trust (RoT) for data-center processors and
accelerators, supporting secure boot, device identity and attestation eMemory
(3529.TW) subsidiary PUFsecurity offers PUFrt, providing NeoPUF-derived device,
NeoFuse OTP secure storage and hardware entropy as physical security building
blocks for Caliptra implementations. Broader adoption could support incremental
PUF/security-IP licensing, although monetization depends on qualifications and

customer design wins. (For more, see our quantum security report).
Other: CXL fabric, cooling and compute ramps: The microchannel lid
demonstration underscored the importance of chip-level thermal design as rack

Key Takeaways
ICA/IPO traction: China CSP and TE demonstrations suggest growing adoption,
although cost could be higher
IMC versus BMC: Marvell IMC complements AST2700; we see no production
disruption.
Hardware security: Caliptra strengthens the case for eMemory PUF-based
security IP.
CXL and thermal: CXL supports memory pooling: Cooling and ASIC/CPU ramps

remain in focus. Muse is likely driving storage server demand.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O or Over) - The stock's total return is expected to exceed the total return of the relevant country MSCI Index or the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry
team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis over the next 12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E or Equal) - The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the total return of the relevant country MSCI Index or the average total return of the analyst's industry (or

industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis over the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR) - Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the relevant country MSCI Index or the average total return of the analyst's
industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U or Under) - The stock's total return is expected to be below the total return of the relevant country MSCI Index or the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry

team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

股價相對大盤走勢

萬海 (2615 TT)  Buy

近洋線進入需求旺季，給予投資建議為 Buy。  
投資建議 ：考量 4Q26 上旬美國線運價相對有撐，近洋線進入需
求旺季，目前 PBR 評價處於近年區間中下緣，給予萬海投資建
議為 Buy ，目標價 140 元。  
近洋線 4Q26 進入需求旺季 ：受惠中國十一長假前的拉貨需求，
颱風因素導致港口作業效率降低，加上巴拿馬運河限制通行船
舶艘數、船舶周轉率下滑等因素影響之下，支撐美國線運價持
續走高；歐洲線運價走勢相對疲弱，主因需求逐漸進入旺季尾
聲，加上部分航線復航紅海、潛在運力增加所致。美國線方面，
10/2026 需求仍相對有撐，隨後將進入傳統需求淡季，而農曆春
節前拉貨需求有望再度回溫。巴拿馬運河先前受到降雨不足影
響而緊縮通航條件， 10/15/2026 開始每日通航預約名額放寬，
通行等候天數增加進而降低美東線船舶周轉率，通航條件改善
後，將進一步減弱對美國線運價的支撐力道。近洋線方面， 4Q26
進入傳統需求旺季，運價有望延續 3Q26 漲勢。   
預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 19.84 元： 1Q26 處於需求淡季，加上市
場預期紅海復航導致運價低迷，萬海 1Q26 營收 336.41 億元，
毛利率由 4Q25 的 23.92% 下滑至 22.76% ，營 業 利 益QoQ-7.86% ，
另業外認列金融資產評價利益，單季稅後獲利 76.72 億元，稅後
EPS 2.73 元。 2Q26 受惠於需求回升、運價調漲，營收
QoQ+27.52% ，毛利率 30.37% ，營業利益 QoQ+91.12% ；業外認
列金融資產評價利益之下 ， 單季稅後獲利 115.35 億元 ， 稅後EPS 
4.11 元。 3Q26 單季營收 QoQ+38.96% ，主因遠洋線為傳統需求
旺季，適逢氣候異常導致亞洲港口塞港、以及巴拿馬運河限航
等因素疊加影響下，支撐美國線運價持續走高，近洋線同樣呈
現上漲趨勢所致 。 預估單季稅後獲利 229.78 億元 ， 稅後EPS 8.19
元。 4Q26 亞洲線進入需求旺季，價量有望齊揚；遠洋線進入需
求淡季，美國線運價走勢將較 3Q26 修正，預估單季稅後獲利
134.88 億元，稅後 EPS 4.81 元。整體而言， 2026 年供過於求壓
力較 2025 年舒緩之下，預估萬海 2026 年稅後獲利 556.72 億
元， YoY+76.94% ，稅後 EPS 19.84 元。

### Page 2

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 10 月 13 日

2 群益投顧 
  公司 ESG 政策：  
(一 )企業對於因應氣候變遷或溫室氣體管理之策略  
1.萬海秉持對環境保護的承諾，除符合 IMO 國際公約規範之下，進行船隊
減排，減少空汙，並且落實  設備保養及改善，確保機器良好運作，以達達
到環保之要求，愛護地球，永續經營。  2. 萬海積極參與國際環保平台，透
過實際行動來推行節能減碳的環保策略：  (1)2015 年參與 Clean Cargo 
Working Group (CCWG) 組織，加強與同業分享環保知識、資訊、技  術的交
流，以推動節能減碳為目的。自 2017 年起依據 CCWG 新規範，萬海提供
CCWG 的船隊相關航  行、油耗數據須由驗證單位檢驗，萬海船隊已連續五
年 (2016 ~ 2021 年度 )排放數據均通過第三方  驗證單位 DNV 之驗證，此將
可主動吸引同為其會員的貨主、承攬業選擇使用符合其環保政策之航商。
(2) 持續參與世界航運協會 (World Shipping Council,WSC) ，針對各項航運事
務及環境保護議題，  進行討論。萬海為該協會會員，副董事長為該協會之
理事會成員。  (3)2019 年加入零排放聯盟 (Getting to ZeroCoalition) ，萬海
持續推動並實現國際航運減少碳  排放為宗旨，致力達成該聯盟目標。  
(二 )企業溫室氣體排放量減量目標  
依照 IMO 規定，以 2008 年為基準降低碳排率，  以 2021 年為止減少 30%
碳排放率，並預計於 2030 年可  以達到 50% 減排率目標，優於 IMO 要求；
未來將持續進行船隊汰舊換新計畫，並配以新型節能主機及  各式最新節能
設備，以達節能減碳、環境保護目標。  
(三 )企業溫室氣體排放量減量之預算與計畫  
1.預算：保護地球本為每一個企業之責任，本公司將以提升船舶性能，降低
船隊油耗，使用低硫燃  油，以降低溫室氣體排放，視該成本為必要性支出。
2. 船舶能源效率管理計畫 (Ship Energy Efficiency Management Plan
(SEEMP) ：  依國際海事組織 (IMO) 之船舶能源效率管理計畫 (Ship Energy 
Efficiency ManagementPlan, SEEMP) 規範訂定有效的能源效率管理制度，並
根據能源效率設計指標 (EnergyEfficient Design Index, EEDI) 作為新船能源
效率的標準，並據實揭露能源效率營運指標 (Energy Efficient Operational
Index, EEOI) 。  3. 萬海另依 IMO 法規要求制定 SEEMP PART II ，加入「燃
油消耗數據收集計畫」 (DataCollection Plan, DCP) ，藉由執行燃油消耗數據
的監測，管理和報告船舶於海上航行時所產生的二氧化碳排  放，並依法規
製作年度排放報告，經第三方查核認證後提交給國際海事組織。  
4Q26 近洋線進入需求旺季：  
3Q26 以來遠洋線走勢呈現分化。受惠中國十一長假前的拉貨需求，颱風因
素導致港口作業效率降低，加上巴拿馬運河限制通行船舶艘數、船舶周轉率
下滑等因素疊加影響之下，支撐美國線運價持續走高；歐洲線運價走勢相對
疲弱，主因需求逐漸進入旺季尾聲，加上部分航線復航紅海、潛在運力增加
所致。  
美國線方面， 10/2026 需求仍相對有撐，隨後將進入傳統需求淡季，而農曆
春節前拉貨需求有望再度回溫。巴拿馬運河先前受到降雨不足影響而緊縮
通航條件， 10/15/2026 開始每日通航預約名額放寬至 33 艘 (正常為 36 艘 )，

3 群益投顧

減弱對美國線運價的支撐力道。

圖一、 SCFI 指數

資料來源：群益投顧彙整  
燃油價格處於高檔震盪：  
油價方面，近期新加坡低硫油價格持續回落。美伊戰爭前約 500 美元 /噸，
戰爭後一度上漲至 1,120 美元 /噸，近期則來到近 890 美元 /噸。以目前運價
水準而言，已完全轉嫁燃油價格變動對營運成本造成的影響。  
新船下水規劃：  
公司至 2025 年底船隊達 116 艘，運力共計 57.27 萬 TEU。 2026 年預計交付
6 艘船舶，包括 2 艘 7,000TEU 及 4 艘 8,700TEU 船舶， 2027 年預計交付 1
艘 8,700TEU 及 10 艘 16,000TEU 船舶。  
營收比重方面， 2025 年美國線比重約 23% 、中東印度線比重約 27% 、亞洲
近洋線比重約 37% 、南美線比重約 13% 。  
預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 19.84 元 : 
1Q26 處於需求淡季，加上市場預期紅海復航導致運價低迷，萬海 1Q26 營
收 336.41 億元，毛利率由 4Q25 的 23.92% 下滑至 22.76% ，營業利益 QoQ-
7.86% ，另業外認列金融資產評價利益，單季稅後獲利 76.72 億元，稅後 EPS
2.73 元。  
2Q26 受惠於需求回升、運價調漲，營收 QoQ+27.52% ，毛利率 30.37% ，營
業利益 QoQ+91.12% ；業外認列金融資產評價利益之下，單季稅後獲利
115.35 億元，稅後 EPS 4.11 元。  
3Q26 單季營收 QoQ+38.96% ，主因遠洋線為傳統需求旺季，適逢氣候異常
導致亞洲港口塞港、以及巴拿馬運河限航等因素疊加影響下，支撐美國線運
價持續走高，近洋線同樣呈現上漲趨勢所致。預估單季稅後獲利 229.78 億
元，稅後 EPS 8.19 元。

7 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌，亦將出具降評報告。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

晟銘電 為伺服器機殼大廠，目前
切入 IT 機櫃、水冷機櫃、水冷搭
配的分歧管 
 晟銘電子科技股份有限公司成立於 1976 年 6 月 ， 為台灣伺服器機殼OEM/ODM
製造商， 公司主要銷售電腦機殼及伺服器機殼 ，其用途為桌上型電腦及伺服器等
各項組件之安裝防護設備 ， 近年來公司積極跨足散熱領域並研發設計出液冷散熱
的浸沒式散熱產品。於 2024 年公司推出多款液冷機櫃及液冷散熱系統。晟銘電
提供全球客戶迅速且彈性的 OEM 及 ODM 生產服務。2026 年 1~8 月主要產品
營收占比，伺服器 73%、L11 系統整合 18%、Tooling 6%、DT 3%。

基本上是跟隨著輝達的腳步走 ， 包括GB300、VR200 等標準品機箱都已經開好

生產基地，另於寧波設有產能，產品以機殼、機櫃為主，東莞廠自動烤漆線預
計今年底前完成，一天可以完成60 個機櫃烤漆；台灣中壢則主要負責打樣及水
冷產品研發，部分零組件仍由中國生產後運送至中壢進行後段組裝。泰國一期
廠已完成建置並具備生產條件 ， 二期則持續進行設備調校 ， 主要導入烤漆產線，
預計 4Q26 開始試產，後續待客戶完成稽核及認證後逐步量產。泰國二期的烤

## Document 270: Continue To Proceed With Caution

Source PDF: all report pdf/Continue To Proceed With Caution.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Continue To Proceed With Caution
Date:    8 hours ago
Continue To Proceed With Caution

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Continue To Proceed With
Caution
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 14 Oct 2026
View in browser
Photo by Muhammad Daudy / Unsplash

I. Risk Off Is Back
On September 15, we advised our readers to "Proceed With Caution." We
postponed our year-end S&P 500 target of 8,400 to mid-2027. Our
concern isn't earnings; it's stock valuations.

### Page 2

We remain bullish on the economy and earnings, expecting no recession
through the end of the decade in 2029. Accordingly, we haven’t lowered
our 2027 S&P 500 earnings forecast of $425 per share, which is only
slightly above the industry analysts' current consensus of $423.08 (chart).
Instead, we lowered our year-end forward P/E forecast from 19.8 to 18.6;
that’s what reduced our year-end S&P 500 target to 7,900. So far, our
caution has been warranted. The S&P 500's forward P/E has been moving

toward our targeted valuation multiple (chart).
Risk-off sentiment was on full display in the stock market today for most of
the reasons we flagged in mid-September. The S&P 500 fell 1.1% to
7,718.94, while the Nasdaq Composite and Russell 2000 both dropped
1.9%. Selling pressure was particularly pronounced among technology

### Page 3

stocks, with the SOXX tumbling 4.5% (chart). Interestingly, IGV fell 3.0%,
while MAGS was down only 1.9%.
The AI trade is getting sloppy amid intensifying competition and doubts
about returns on massive AI investments. Shares of heavily indebted
neocloud provider CoreWeave plunged 16% today amid growing concerns
about its ability to service its debt (chart). Furthermore, today's CPI was
hotter than expected, raising the odds of two Fed rate hikes over the rest

of this year. A cargo ship sank in the Strait of Hormuz after hitting an
Iranian mine.
Meanwhile, the risk-off sentiment we anticipated may be helping
Treasuries find their footing. On Saturday, we suggested that the 10-year
Treasury yield might stabilize around 5.25%. So far, that call is holding up

### Page 4

despite mounting fears of further Fed tightening today (chart). That's partly
because Treasuries offer attractive real returns, with the 10-year TIPS
yielding around 2.90% (chart).
Our worry list remains long, including more partisanship after the midterm
elections and a re-escalation of the war in the Middle East. We expect
risk-off sentiment to persist, further compressing valuation multiples.
That's why we still recommend proceeding with caution. On the other

hand, buying opportunities are increasing, and Santa could still deliver a
year-end rally up to 7,900.
II. Inflation Is Back Too
Today's September CPI report fueled bets on further Fed tightening. The
probability of an October rate hike jumped from 21% to 36%, briefly
reaching 45%. Meanwhile, the 2-year Treasury yield surged as much as
13bps intraday before closing 3bps higher at 4.79%, roughly 95 bps above
the midpoint of the federal funds target range (chart).

### Page 5

Here's more:
(1) CPI inflation (m/m). The headline CPI jumped 0.6% m/m in
September, its biggest increase since March, led by a 9.3% surge in
energy prices (chart). But inflationary pressures were broad-based, with
the core CPI rising 0.3%, including gains of 0.3% in core goods and 0.4%
in core services. Indeed, 52% of CPI categories recorded inflation above
3% y/y, with 70% above 2%.
Durable goods prices jumped 0.9% m/m in September (chart). Notably,

prices of computers, peripherals, and smart home assistants rose 1.3%,
pushing this three-month moving average inflation rate to a record high.
That reflects surging semiconductor prices driven by the AI investment

### Page 6

boom. Tariffs are also adding to inflation, with prices of other household
equipment and furnishings jumping 2.1% m/m.
Nondurable goods prices jumped 1.4% m/m in September, led by a 9.3%
surge in motor fuel prices (chart).
The services sector also showed broad-based inflationary pressures, with
prices rising 0.4% m/m (chart). Higher energy costs are passing through
to transportation and travel services, while robust aggregate demand is
sustaining pricing power.

### Page 7

(2) CPI inflation (y/y). Both headline and core inflation accelerated on a
y/y basis in September, marking the 67th consecutive month that inflation
has remained above the Fed's 2% target (chart).
Both goods and services inflation rose in September (chart). With durable
goods deflation unlikely to return soon, services inflation must fall more to
bring inflation back to target. However, services inflation remains
stubbornly elevated.

### Page 8

Supercore CPI inflation rose to 3.2% y/y in September, underscoring the
persistence of underlying price pressures (chart).
III. The Fed's Beige Book
The Fed's October Beige Book, which summarizes economic conditions
across the 12 Federal Reserve districts, reinforced the September CPI
report's message that inflation remains troublesome.
Half the districts reported robust or accelerating price pressures,
compared with broadly stable inflation in September. Four districts

reported accelerating inflation, while three characterized price growth as
robust, up from one previously. Higher energy costs, tariffs, and insurance
premiums were the main drivers, with diesel costs passing through via

### Page 9

freight surcharges (chart). Expiring hedges and contract renewals threaten
further increases.
Meanwhile, economic activity remains robust, supported by strong
manufacturing demand from AI data centers, defense, and energy.
💡

log in to the Yardeni QuickTakes website and post them at the end
of the QuickTakes article. Paid members' contributions may be
featured in our segment, "Ed Answers Your Questions".

clause.

## Document 272: CoreWeave债券暴跌敲响警钟，美联储或需重新审视加息节奏

Source PDF: all report pdf/CoreWeave债券暴跌敲响警钟，美联储或需重新审视加息节奏.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

CoreWeave 债券收益率⼀度飙⾄ 15% ，垃圾级和投资级收益率分别创 2025 年和 2023 年以
来新⾼。彭博专栏指出， 2028 年⾼收益债到期⾼峰逼近，若信⽤利差⽆序扩张恐引发违约
潮与失业上升，美联储应在 10 ⽉会议按兵不动，维持 3.75% ⾄ 4% 利率区间，审慎权衡 12 ⽉
决策。
摘要：
沉睡多年的企业信贷市场正在苏醒，其释放的信号令美联储⾯临两难处境。
云计算服务商 CoreWeave 的债券本⽉遭遇重挫，其 2032 年到期票据收益率⼀度飙升⾄ 15% ，垃
圾级和投资级债券收益率也分别升⾄ 2025 年和 2023 年以来最⾼⽔平。这标志着企业融资成本这
⼀货币政策传导的关键渠道，正在重新发挥抑制经济增⻓的作⽤ —— ⽽这⼀效应在过去数年间
⼏近失灵。
美联储已于 9 ⽉重启加息。专栏作家 Jonathan Levin 在彭博撰⽂指出，此番企业信贷成本的上升
固然是美联储⻓期以来所期待的紧缩效果，但并不意味着决策者能完全掌控后续⾛向。在 2028
年⾼收益债券到期⾼峰逼近的背景下，若信⽤利差出现⽆序扩张，可能引发⼤规模违约潮和失

业上升，进⽽迅速侵蚀近年积累的居⺠财富。鉴于此，美联储应在 10 ⽉议息会议上按兵不动，
维持 3.75% ⾄ 4% 的利率区间不变，并审慎权衡 12 ⽉的决策。
企业融资盾牌消退，信贷传导重新激活
过去数年，美国企业凭借疫情期间锁定的超低借贷成本，抵御了美联储⾃ 2022 年以来最激进的
加息周期，令投资者和经济学家⼤感意外。彼时⼤量企业以极低利率发⾏⻓期债券或完成贷款
再融资，当联储开始加息时，这些企业的实际融资成本⼏乎未受冲击。
据美国经济分析局数据，从 2021 年初⾄ 2022 年底，企业实际上是加息预期的净受益者 —— 原因
在于，其持有的现⾦储备收益随利率上升⽽快速增厚，⽽债务端的重定价则极为缓慢。在随后
五年⾥，⾮⾦融企业净利息⽀出仅增加约 860 亿美元，⽽税前利润却同期激增约 1.2 万亿美元⾄
约 2.7 万亿美元。
这⼀局⾯从根本上切断了货币政策传导链条中的关键⼀环 —— 即通过提⾼资本成本来抑制企业
投资与招聘。此情形与 1400 万⼾家庭在利率 2% ⾄ 3% 时完成房贷再融资的 " 抵押贷款锁定效应 " 如

出⼀辙。⽽如今，利息⽀出对企业经营的影响正在骤然回归。
CoreWeave 是极端案例，却映射更⼴泛⻛险
CoreWeave 的商业模式尤为依赖持续进⼊债务市场融资：通过不断借款购置 AI 芯⽚、扩建数据
中⼼，再以⻓期合约形式出租算⼒，以此赚取超越融资成本的回报。这⼀模式的前提是能以合
理利率稳定获取资⾦，⼀旦融资条件收紧，整个逻辑链便告断裂。
上周 CoreWeave 债券与股价双双崩跌，彭博报道称并⽆明显单⼀催化剂，主因是⻓期国债收益
率整体⾛⾼。市场研究机构 Gartner Inc. 同期发布报告，指出 AI 收⼊或难以⽀撑当前数据中⼼投
资规模，进⼀步加剧了市场悲观情绪。
这场冲击正在向更⼴范围蔓延。 Oracle 、 Alphabet 和 Meta 等企业的借贷成本均因美国国债收益
率上升及投资者要求更⾼⻛险溢价⽽⾛⾼。当前美国财政⾚字约占 GDP 的 6% 且预计维持⾼位，
国债收益率⼤幅下⾏的空间⼗分有限。

据彭博美国⾼收益债券指数，约四分之⼀成分债券将在未来⼀⾄三年内到期，占⽐接近历史最
⾼⽔平。⾼企的收益率对于需在 2028 年前后集中再融资的发⾏⼈⽽⾔将构成严峻考验，彼时到
期压⼒墙最为险峻。
投资级市场所受冲击相对可控。对于回报率达 50% 的 AI 项⽬⽽⾔，⽆论投资级收益率是 5% 还是
6% ，推进决策不会受到根本动摇；但对于那些盈利前景不确定、回报率较低的项⽬，若⾼融资
成本持续，相关投资计划或将被搁置。
Jonathan Levin 指出，货币政策历来存在 " 漫⻓且多变的滞后效应 " ，但此轮尤为特殊：企业信贷
传导渠道失灵的持续时间，是数⼗年来罕⻅的。与此同时，⾼达 78 万亿美元的居⺠净财富增量
以及战时规模的财政⾚字，也在持续缓冲紧缩政策对增⻓的冲击，令货币政策的实际效果更加

难以预判。
良性紧缩与失序⻛险，⼀线之隔
在新任主席沃什的领导下，美联储⾯临⼀个微妙的政策窗⼝。温和的信⽤收紧，有望在半导体
及数据中⼼相关供给缺⼝逐步弥合的同时，适度冷却 AI 资本⽀出热潮，从⽽在不引发系统性⻛险
的前提下，推动通胀在近六年偏离⽬标后重回 2% 。
但若信⽤利差出现⽆序扩张， 2028 年的债务到期⾼峰将从 " 具有挑战性 " 演变为 " 难以为继 " ，进
⽽触发连锁违约和失业上升，并迅速侵蚀多年积累的居⺠财富。
两种情景究竟哪⼀种会成为现实，⽬前尚⽆定论。正是这种不确定性，要求美联储在 10 ⽉议息
会议上维持利率于 3.75% ⾄ 4% 区间，并对 12 ⽉的决策保持审慎。 CoreWeave 债券的暴跌已将沉
睡的信贷市场唤醒，这头巨兽在未来数⽉将如何反应，⽆⼈能够预知。

## Document 323: Insurance costs hit the brakes Insurance payment growth is cooling, but the relief consumers are seeing depends heavily on who and where they are. 2 min read

Source PDF: all report pdf/Insurance costs hit the brakes Insurance payment growth is cooling, but the relief consumers are seeing depends heavily on who and where they are. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13028742     1

Economy 
Insurance costs hit the brakes 
16 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Property insurance (e.g., auto, homeowners, renters) payment growth has slowed considerably, according to Bank of America 
payments data. Median payments rose just 1% year-over-year (YoY) in 2026, easing further from the 6% growth seen in 2025, 
after a roughly 12% increase in 2024, suggesting that many consumers are getting some relief from rising insurance payments. 
• However, the moderation is uneven across consumers. Bank of America payments data suggests that Gen Z continues to 
experience insurance payment growth of nearly 6%, while increases for other generations are closer to 1% to 2%. Payments 
also rose nearly 1.5% for higher-income households but edged lower for those with lower incomes. 
• Lower accident rates, easing vehicle prices, and softer major-purchase growth may be reducing cost pressures, but geography 
remains a major dividing line. Florida metros recorded declines of roughly 7% to 8%, while California and several Northeastern 
metros continued to see relatively strong increases.

Insurance payments stabilize after years of painful growth 
The burden of higher property (e.g., auto, renters, homeowners) insurance payments may be easing for some. Notably, while the 
average payment increased 3% year-over-year (YoY), the median payment was only up 1%, according to Bank of America 
payments data. This suggests that most consumers have only seen a small increase in their premiums, though there may be a 
few outliers (Exhibit 1).  
The latest data stands in contrast to just two years ago in 2024, when most consumers saw their insurance payments surge 
about 12% YoY, after a nearly 8% YoY increase in 2023.   
Exhibit 1: Insurance payments have increased only modestly through August of this year, with growth peaking in 2024 
Median and average insurance payments (September to August yearly, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Insurance payments by age show a similar pattern, with much stronger growth in 2024 across most generations followed by a 
cooling since then (Exhibit 2). Gen Z is the outlier here, with their payments still growing at a healthy rate, increasing almost 6% 
YoY for both 2026 and 2025, after a 7% increase in 2024. Meanwhile, Millennials and Baby Boomers experienced a 2% YoY 
increase so far this year, with Gen X only seeing around a 1% rise.

Bank of America payments data by income provides even more insight. Higher-income households paid nearly 1.5% more in 
2026 than they did a year ago, while middle-income households saw only modest increases or none at all (Exhibit 3). Conversely, 
lower-income households actually paid slightly less so far this year than they did last year.  
Exhibit 3: Higher-income households are still paying more for insurance compared to last year, while lower earners are paying less 
Median insurance payments by income cuts (September to August yearly, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data 
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Fewer accidents and lower vehicles prices may be driving insurance payments down 
But why might insurance payments be declining at all? One thing to keep in mind is that insurance companies often raise rates 
to cover events that occurred in previous years to recover costs, while adjusting for changing risks.  
For example, the crash rate – the number of vehicle crashes per one million miles driven – suggests that there was a sharp rise 
in accidents in 2021, according to the Department of Transportation (Exhibit 4). Additionally, new and used vehicle prices 
increased significantly after the pandemic, according to Consumer Price Index (CPI) data from the Bureau of Labor Statistics 
(BLS) (Exhibit 5). So, insurers were not only facing more vehicle replacements due to a higher crash rate, but a higher cost to 
replace vehicles as well.

However, the crash rate mostly declined from 2021 to 2024, and the Department of Transportation reported that the number of 
accidents likely decreased again in 2025. Also, while the cost to maintain and repair vehicles has consistently grown since 2021, 
the price to replace vehicles peaked in 2022 and has eased since then – the latter likely a more important factor in insurance 
costs than the former. Additionally, while the CPI index for motor vehicle insurance has fallen 5% YoY, it’s possible that this may 
partially be due to rebates, according to BofA Global Research. So, the price figured into CPI may not be fully realized by the 
consumer.

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Another factor in the divergence between a declining CPI and rising insurance payments in our data may be the growth in the 
amount of property people are insuring now. Notably though, this growth has eased in recent years. YoY growth in both new 
home and total vehicle sales peaked in mid-2023 and has grown modestly, or even declined, since (Exhibit 6). And given that 
these major purchases are still K-shaped (read more in Autos: In gear, but the “K” is still in the headlights and On the move: 
Renovation over relocation?), it could help to explain the divergence in insurance payment growth across income cohorts.  
Exhibit 6: Growth in new home and total vehicle sales peaked in mid-2023 due to a post-pandemic boom but has eased since

Florida’s metros have seen the largest decline when compared to other major cities 
Examining different cities across the US also sheds light on insurance trends. Consumers in major California metros saw the 
largest increases in 2026, following near double digit growth in 2025, likely driven by widespread property damage from 
wildfires, according to BofA Global Research (Exhibit 7). Major coastal metros in the Northeast like Boston and New York also 
experienced sizable increases as well.  
Conversely, many cities throughout the Sun Belt like Houston, Nashville and Dallas saw flat or even declining insurance 
payments. BofA Global Research points to several reasons for these declines: part of the equation in this region is that the 
massive runup in the cost of property insurance and domestic migration to the region through 2024 has since eased or reversed 
course.   
Exhibit 7: California major metros have seen steady insurance payment growth, while Miami metros have seen declines 
Median insurance payments by core based statistical areas (September to August yearly, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
In Florida, legislation that went into effect in 2023, eventually made its way into insurance prices in 2025, leading to widespread 
declines in premiums. The state also has a “lookback period” of three years, where insurance profits can be deemed too high and 
a rebate is issued to consumers. It’s possible this rebate is factoring into declines this year, as some carriers only allow rebates 
upon renewals.  
The Sunshine State was a notable standout for declines, with Orlando, Tampa and Jacksonville each down around 8% YoY in 
2026, followed closely by Miami with a 7% decrease. Additionally, Bank of America payments data suggests that Jacksonville, 
Miami and Orlando all had median insurance payments above the national level in 2023, though their payments were fell below 
that in 2026 (Exhibit 8).  
Major metros in Texas also saw easing insurance payments relative to 2023, while Seattle, San Jose, and Riverside, CA are 
among the major metros that went from below the national median to above in the three-year period ending in 2026.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Insurance payments include renters, auto or home, but do not include insurance escrowed into mortgage payments or deducted from a 
paycheck.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts. 
The “more discretionary” retail category includes furniture, electronics, building materials, online marketplaces, clothing retailers and 
miscellaneous stores; while “partly discretionary” retail contains auto parts and services, health and personal care retailers, general merchandise 
and “mostly necessity”  retail contains gas and groceries. 
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

### Page 4

◆ The economy remains on the recovery track despite weakness in some
areas
Annualized real GDP growth in the second quarter was +1.1% QoQ, with growth
staying in positive territory for three straight quarters. Although personal
consumption exhibited weakness and capital investment declined, overall growth
was lifted by foreign demand. Exports, particularly of services, increased, and the
drop in imports of Middle Eastern crude oil also helped foreign demand turn positive.

◆ Housing starts are weak
Housing starts surged 18.6% YoY in June. They have continued to rise on a YoY
basis, but the jump was mainly due to a rebound from the slump in the same month
of the previous year. The recovery in the number of starts is actually moderate,
reflecting higher interest rates and soaring construction costs.
◆ Earthquake impact will need to be watched
Damage from the earthquake that occurred in Kumamoto Prefecture at the end of

July is weighing on economic activity in the affected areas. Although moves toward
recovery can be seen, with many companies having already resumed operations,
the impact could become prolonged in the following scenarios:
The first is if damage to capital stock such as factories and roads has been severe.
Some companies have sustained damage to production facilities, making an early
resumption of operations difficult in some cases.
The second is if a downturn in tourism demand occurs. According to some media

reports, there has been an uptick in cancellations of accommodation reservations in
Kumamoto Prefecture and neighboring prefectures since the earthquake. After the
2016 Kumamoto earthquake, the decline in travel demand from Japanese visitors
was short lived, but lower demand from foreign tourists persisted for a long period.
Annualized real GDP growth was +1.1% quarter over quarter (QoQ) in Q2 2026

### Page 5

Monthly Report of Prospects for Japan's Economy August 2026
The Japan Research Institute, Limited
Exports and inbound demand remain solid despite weakness in some areas
◆ Goods exports are on the verge of picking up
Goods exports are treading water. A sharp rise in memory chip prices is among
factors pushing up export prices, resulting in record high levels of exports in value
terms recently. On the other hand, the increase in volume terms is limited. One

possible reason is that Japan's export competitiveness has declined in fields such as
semiconductors, where global demand is growing.
Looking ahead, exports are expected to pick up. Capital investment by chipmakers
is expanding worldwide, and this should lift exports of semiconductor manufacturing
equipment, an area in which Japan has strength. However, continued caution is
needed regarding the effects of prolonged turmoil in the Middle East. A global

slowdown due to elevated crude oil prices could weigh on exports.
◆ Reluctance to visit among Chinese travelers is weighing on demand for
travel to Japan
Inbound demand is currently soft. The Chinese government's advisory urging its
citizens to refrain from traveling to Japan is having an adverse impact. The number
of Chinese visitors to Japan has fallen to about half the level prior to the advisory.
Meanwhile, the number of tourists from countries other than China remains firm.

Inbound demand is expected to stay robust going forward. Continued increases in
tourists from countries other than China will remain a supporting factor. The
expectation that the yen will remain weak will also act to push up inbound
consumption. However, there are two downside risks going forward:
The first is that the Chinese government keeps the advisory in place for a
prolonged period. When China banned group travel to South Korea in 2017, tourist

numbers stayed low for two to three years.
The second is the impact of higher crude oil prices. Major airlines have embarked
on phased increases in fuel surcharges, which could weigh on inbound demand
during the summer.

### Page 6

Personal consumption is gradually recovering thanks to a favorable income environment
◆ Higher disposable incomes are supporting personal consumption
Personal consumption continues to gradually recover. While consumption of
non-durable goods remains weak, services consumption is firm, particularly in
amusement-related services. In addition, consumption of durable goods has
increased significantly. Temporary factors such as the abolition of the environmental

performance tax on automobiles (since April 2026) and the tightening of
energy-efficiency standards for air conditioners (effective April 2027) have also
contributed to this rise.
A favorable income environment can also be pointed to as a background factor
behind the recovery in personal consumption. YoY real disposable income growth has
generally been positive since mid-2024, and the pace of increase has recently
accelerated. Employee remuneration is rising, partly as a result of agreements made

during the shunto (spring wage negotiations), and net asset income is also increasing
at a faster clip as companies hike dividends in line with stock price rises.
◆ Stock price developments could be a source of concern for personal
consumption
Personal consumption is expected to continue its gradual recovery going forward. In
addition to the favorable income environment and government policy measures to
curb price increases, a recovery in consumer confidence driven by easing tensions in

the Middle East will also provide a tailwind. Recently, the Consumer Confidence Index
has been showing signs of bottoming out.
However, a reverse wealth effect stemming from falling stock prices is a potential
cause for concern. The Nikkei Stock Average, which reached a new high in June, has
been struggling to push higher since the start of July. Estimates suggest the wealth
effect is concentrated among elderly households, but in recent years working-age

households have also increased their holdings of equity-related assets, and the
positive wealth effect for them may have supported consumption to some extent. For
this reason, if stock prices were to fall significantly, there is a danger of downward
pressure on consumption across a wide range of households.

セン

◆ The unemployment rate has settled in the mid-2% range
While the employment situation is generally favorable, weakness can be seen in
some areas. The unemployment rate has remained in the mid-2% range since 2022.
The number of employed persons in non-manufacturing, where business conditions
are favorable, is holding firm, while in manufacturing the number remains at a level
below that seen before the COVID-19 pandemic.
In the background to the drop in manufacturing employment is a slump in

production activity. Manufacturing output has been shrinking due to the effects of
U.S. tariff policy, soaring raw material prices, and a decline in the international
competitiveness of Japanese firms. As a result, pressure to reduce headcounts has
been building. Even in non-manufacturing, some industries closely linked to
manufacturing, such as transportation and wholesale/retail, are also exhibiting
weakness in labor demand.
◆ The employment situation could remain fragile in the near term

Looking ahead at the employment situation in the short term, a sense of
stagnation may persist. The contraction in output in the manufacturing sector will
continue to weigh on labor demand. Although production activity has been brisker
recently, particularly in semiconductor-related industries, the employment-creation
capacity of such industries is small. The employment-inducement coefficient per unit
of new demand tends to be lower than in other sectors, meaning that the industries

lack the strength to serve as drivers of employment.
However, in the medium to long term, the unemployment rate is expected to see a
gradual decline. Demand for labor is projected to stay solid, especially in
face-to-face services such as medical/nursing care and accommodation and
eating/drinking services, but the number of people seeking employment from the
non-labor force population has declined significantly due to the expansion in elderly
and female workforce participation. With limited room for expansion in the labor

supply, the job market is expected to tighten further.

The government is curbing inflation
◆ Policy factors are pushing down the inflation rate
In June, core inflation (all items, less fresh food) was 1.6%, staying under 2%
for the fifth consecutive month. Food price inflation has slowed, and a major

reason for this is a fallback in rice prices, which soared last year. Gasoline
subsidies are also continuing to curtail energy price increases.
Inflation looks set to hover in the 2% range in the second half of 2026. Although
energy subsidies and other government measures will soften inflationary pressure
to some extent, with imported inflationary pressure building, prices are expected
to be pushed up as higher costs are passed through to selling prices. From

FY2027 onward, the reduction in the consumption tax rate for food items will
serve to keep prices down, and inflation is expected to drop into the 1% range.
◆ The Bank of Japan (BOJ) will keep its policy rate unchanged
At its July monetary policy meeting, the BOJ decided to keep its policy rate
unchanged.
Long-term interest rates remain high. This reflects concerns about inflation
resulting from the increase in oil prices and expectations of an expansion in fiscal

expenditure.
The BOJ is expected to step up the pace of interest rate hikes while closely
monitoring the impact of higher rates on the real economy and financial/capital
markets. The next rate hike is expected to be in September.
Long-term interest rates look likely to rise gradually, reflecting heightened
anticipation of rate hikes by the BOJ and increased fiscal spending by the
government. If jitters about a deterioration in fiscal discipline resulting from such

measures as energy subsidies or the consumption tax cut take hold, there is also
a risk that long-term interest rates will rise.

◆ Surging crude oil prices have become a source of upward pressure on
prices
With crude oil prices having spiked against the backdrop of the worsening situation
in the Middle East, corporate input costs have risen sharply, and pressure for price
pass-through is intensifying. Looking at the Final Demand-Intermediate Demand
price indexes, the margin of price increases at the upstream stage far exceeds that at
the downstream stage, and price pass-through pressure is also substantial at the

downstream stage.
According to estimates based on past data, the impact of rising oil prices involves
a time lag. Specifically, the push-up effect on the consumer price inflation rate
increases for around 10 months and continues to exert influence for a long period
thereafter.
◆ Price pass-through willingness is also stronger than before
Notably, food product manufacturers are planning to raise prices for a huge
number of items during the summer and beyond. According to Teikoku Databank, the

number of items slated for price increases as of the end of July, when it conducted its
latest survey, was higher than in the May and June surveys.
If government subsidies for electricity/gas bills and gasoline prices are scaled back
or ended, both energy and food prices will increase concurrently, and the consumer
price inflation rate may rise again.
Attention also needs to be paid to the possibility that price pass-through driven by
elevated oil prices will become prolonged. In the 2020s, the rate of price increases in

transactions between companies has been higher than in the 2010s, and firms'
medium-term outlooks for selling prices have also shifted upward. Events that trigger
cost increases, such as heightened geopolitical tensions, have been occurring
repeatedly, and companies may be more willing to pass through higher costs to
prices than they were before.

### Page 10

The economy is expected to continue to recover, but downside risks are present
◆ The economy is projected to stay on the recovery track
The Japanese economy should continue its gradual recovery. WTI crude oil
futures prices are expected to fall moderately going forward.
In the corporate sector, government support for investment is providing a tailwind,
and demand for investment for labor saving and digital transformation remains
robust. In addition, rising global demand for AI-related goods should support exports.

Supply chain disruptions resulting from the deterioration in the Middle East situation
will begin to ease, and corporate production activity is expected to gradually recover.
In the household sector, inflationary pressure is expected to strengthen from the
summer onward due to higher crude oil prices. In response, the government will
probably ease the inflationary pressure through measures such as energy subsidies.
While the pace of increase in personal consumption is likely to slow somewhat,

consumption is expected to remain resilient amid continued high wage growth. From
FY2027 onward, the reduction in the consumption tax rate for food items should also
support personal consumption.
◆ The Middle East situation and rising interest rates constitute risks
Real GDP growth is projected to remain solid, registering +0.8% in FY2026 and
+0.9% in FY2027.
However, if the situation in the Middle East worsens again or becomes prolonged,
there is a risk that downward pressure on the economy will intensify, mainly through

three channels: 1) weaker exports, 2) raw material supply constraints, and 3) rising
prices. In addition, if market concerns about the fiscal stance of the Takaichi
government grow, the yen could weaken further, and long-term interest rates could
rise, potentially forcing the scaling-back or scrapping of expansionary policies such
as investment support measures, energy subsidies, and lower consumption tax.
Monthly Report of Prospects for Japan's Economy August 2026

The Japan Research Institute, Limited- ８ -

## Document 332: Kicking and screaming_ Child care costs persist Child care remains a constraint for many families’ budgets, with the average share of monthly child care 8% of a household’s pay. 2 

Source PDF: all report pdf/Kicking and screaming_ Child care costs persist Child care remains a constraint for many families’ budgets, with the average share of monthly child care 8% of a household’s pay. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13030323     1

Economy 
Kicking and screaming: Child care costs persist 
20 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Child care remains a growing financial burden for many households, even as growth in the median payment has moderated, 
according to Bank of America payments data. More families are paying more than $2,000 per month for care, suggesting 
affordability pressures are becoming increasingly concentrated among households facing the highest expenses. 
• Child care cost pressures vary significantly across the country. Nashville and Jacksonville, FL posted the fastest year-over-year 
growth among major metro areas, while New York and Washington DC saw the biggest decrease in median payment growth, 
according to Bank of America payments data. 
• Limited supply may continue to constrain access to child care for some households. Child care employment fell 0.7% year-over-
year in September, suggesting provider capacity remains under pressure and could continue to affect household finances and 
labor force participation.

Child care costs outpace rent and medical services 
Child care remains a significant expense for many families. In September, the growth in child care and other school-related fees 
surpassed other major household expenses such as rent and medical services, according to the Bureau of Labor Statistics (BLS) 
(Exhibit 1). 
Yet, while child care prices continue to rise in official inflation data, median household payments have largely moderated since 
February, according to Bank of America data (Exhibit 2). More importantly, the growth in the number of households paying for 
child care has fallen since the start of 2025. 
Exhibit 1: The cost of child care has surpassed other services such as 
rent in the consumer price index  
Consumer price index by category (monthly, seasonally adjusted, YoY%)

Source: Haver Analytics  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Exhibit 2: The median child care payment per household was up 
1.0% year-over-year (YoY) in September but the number of 
households paying for child care fell 1.0% YoY 
Median child care payment per household and number of households 
(monthly, three-month moving average, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

Fewer lower-income households are paying for child care 
Why is Bank of America payment growth trending in the opposite direction of the Consumer Price Index? Households are likely 
changing the amount of care they use, which is reflected in slower growth in the number paying for child care. As more

households shift into lower-cost care (school-age, public pre-K, subsidized or free after-school programs), median payments can 
fall even as prices continue rising.  
The decline in the number of households paying for child care may also reflect families seeking alternatives to formal care 
arrangements. Some may be relying more heavily on relatives, shifting parents’ work schedules or dropping out of the workforce 
altogether to care for their children in response to elevated costs. This could explain why, among all income groups paying for 
care, the number of lower-income households has seen the biggest decline, falling 2.0% year-over-year (YoY) in September 
(Exhibit 3). 
Exhibit 3: The number of lower-income households paying for child care has fallen the most, down 2.0% YoY in September 
Number of households with child care payments (monthly, three-month moving average, YoY%)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
More families face larger monthly bills 
Yet even as fewer households pay for child care, the share of those shelling out more than $2,000 per month has increased 
substantially (Exhibit 4). In fact, it has more than doubled since 2023, reaching 7.7% in 2026 year-to-date. Meanwhile, the share 
of those paying $1,000 or less has declined, suggesting higher monthly payments are becoming more common even as the 
national median grows more slowly. 
Exhibit 4: The share of households paying >$2000 a month for child care has more than doubled since 2023 
Monthly child care payments by amount (annual average, share of households, %)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Rising costs take a bigger bite out of households’ paychecks 
What makes child care “affordable”? According to the U.S. Department of Health and Human Services, child care is considered 
affordable if it accounts for no more than 7% of family income. Yet, in Bank of America data, the average child care cost was 8% 
in September.  
While an 8% share may not appear dramatically above the federal affordability benchmark of 7%, it represents an average across 
households’ income levels. For families in high-cost metro areas or those paying more than $2,000 per month, the burden is 
likely considerably higher.

Notably, lower- and middle-income households see outsized shares of their pay dedicated to child care in Bank of America 
payments data, though this has moderated slightly from 2025 (Exhibit 5).  
Exhibit 5: In September, the average child care payment was 8.6% of lower- and middle-income total households’ pay, whereas for higher-income 
families it accounted for 7.3% 
Average child care payment as a share of pay in September by income (annual, %)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Where child care costs are climbing 
Child care pressures also differ substantially by location. In Bank of America payments data, Nashville posted the fastest YoY 
median payment growth in September, followed by Jacksonville, FL and Phoenix (Exhibit 6). This dispersion of growth across the 
country suggests child care cost pressures are no longer confined to historically expensive coastal markets.  
Interestingly, New York and Washington DC saw the sharpest decline in growth this year. Why might this be? The supply side of 
the market may help explain why – for example, New York City recently introduced universal Pre-K.1

  I NSTI TUTE

Fewer providers, higher costs 
As of September 2026, employment in child care services was down 0.7% from a year earlier, suggesting the sector’s workforce 
recovery has stalled. Fewer workers can constrain the number of available places or the hours providers offer. That may leave 
families facing longer searches, fewer workable arrangements or continued price pressure. In turn, access to care can influence 
parents’ ability to enter, remain in or expand their participation in the workforce. 
Exhibit 7: Employment within child care services fell 0.7% YoY in September 
Employees by sector (monthly, seasonally adjusted, YoY%)

Source: Haver Analytics  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
What are the economic implications of rising child care costs? 
Child care costs may have important labor market implications for families with young children. Of those households paying for 
child care in 2026, more than half of them were Older Millennials (52%), followed by Younger Millennials (20%) and Gen X (16%), 
according to Bank of America payments data. This means child care costs weigh more heavily on Older Millennials. 
The share of Older Millennial households receiving more than one paycheck has continued to grow from a year earlier, but the 
pace of that improvement has moderated over the past three months (Exhibit 8). Given that this group accounts for the majority 
of households paying for child care in Bank of America payments data, they may offer early signs of how affordability affects 
their employment decisions. 
Exhibit 8: The share of Older Millennial households paying for child care that have more than one person with a paycheck has declined since 2021  
Share of households with a child care payment with more than one person with a paycheck (%, monthly, three-month moving average)

Source: Bank of America internal data  
BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE 
Child care challenges can weigh on women’s employment 
According to data from the American Time Use Survey, women continue to perform significantly more child care and household 
work than men, even as their participation in paid work has increased. This suggests that persistent gaps in access and 
affordability may have disproportionate consequences for women’s employment. Indeed, the percentage of women who cited 
child care as a reason for temporary unemployment was nearly three times higher than men’s in 2026, though the average fell 
from 2025, according to data from the BLS. 
Yet labor force participation data indicates a more nuanced picture. Despite persistent child care pressures, mothers are 
participating in the workforce at rates that remain above their 2019 averages.

Methodology 
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Bank of America credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card holders 
making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards is excluded. Data regarding 
merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial services 
companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System (NAICS), 
which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
If applicable, the consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level.  
If applicable, any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a 
deposit account or credit card. Any reference to aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-
peer, cash and checks.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The six-month annualised growth rate in the Westpac–
Melbourne Institute Leading Index, which indicates the
likely pace of economic activity relative to trend three to
nine months into the future, held at –0.03% in September,
unchanged from August.

The Leading Index continues to show moderately improved
momentum compared to the middle of the year, with the
latest reads pointing to growth holding at or slightly below
trend heading into late 2026/early 2027. Growth appears
to be stabilising at a slow pace rather than stalling. That
said, both the latest fuel prices and the RBA’s September
interest rate increase are still working their way through to
the economy with impacts likely to take some of the gloss off

growth near term. Overall, Westpac expects annual growth
to come in at 1.5% for calendar 2026, a below-trend result.
The Leading Index growth rate has firmed slightly over the
last six months, rising from –0.16% back in March to –0.03%
in September. The component detail shows mixed moves over
the period. Four components have contributed more positively
with reduced drags from the Westpac-Melbourne Institute
Unemployment Expectations Index (adding +0.23ppts to

the Index growth rate); the Westpac-Melbourne Institute
Consumer Expectations Index (adding another +0.15ppts);
and the S&P/ASX200 (+0.15ppts) combining with a modest
lift in growth in US industrial production (adding +0.12ppts).
These positives have been partially offset by modest drags
from four previously stable components: dwelling approvals
(–0.34ppts); monthly hours worked (–0.08ppts); commodity
prices, measured in AUD terms (–0.07ppts) and the yield

spread (–0.05ppts).
Overall, the component mix speaks to a lack of direction.
Indeed, despite some large shocks over the last year, the
Leading Index growth rate has been relatively contained since
early 2025, holding in a narrow –0.3% to +0.5% range. This
compares to history which, outside of the COVID and GFC
shocks, has seen growth rates move between a much wider
–1.9% to +1.6% range. Of course, the apparent stability,
which is evident across all components of the index, may not

last but for now it suggests the economy has been stuck in a
bit of a holding pattern over the last two years.
Leading Index still tracking a touch below trend
The Westpac-Melbourne Institute Leading Index of
Economic Activity is designed to assist in identifying
turning points in the economy. The index combines
variables that reflect different aspects of the economy
into a single index that generally produces a more reliable
cyclical indicator than any single component taken

individually. The Leading Index of Economic Activity
provides advance information on the state of the economy
and gives early warnings of cyclical turning points.
Matthew Hassan
Head of Australian Macro-Forecasting
**Please note that this will be the last Westpac press release for
the monthly Westpac-Melbourne Institute Leading Index. We will
continue to publish regular bulletin updates through our westpaciq.
com.au website.
